\frac{\frac{\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\mathbf{1}_{m}){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathbf{1}_{m}){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\mathbf{1}_{m})\oplus\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathbf{1}_{m}){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Plus}{{Plus}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1}+C_{2},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}

•

C=\texttt{assume}~e
, and
e
must either be a test
b
or a weight
u\in U
. Suppose that
e
is a test
b
. Now, for any
m
define the operator
b\textsf{?}m
as follows:

b\textsf{?}m(\sigma)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\dsser{0}&\text{if}&\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket(\sigma)=\dsser{0}\\
m(\sigma)&\text{if}&\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket(\sigma)=\dsser{1}\end{array}\right.

Therefore
m=b\textsf{?}m+\lnot b\textsf{?}m
and
\mathbf{1}_{m}=\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}\oplus\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}
. We also have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~b,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~b\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~b\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(b\textsf{?}m+\lnot b\textsf{?}m)\mid m\in\varphi\}

\displaystyle=\{b\textsf{?}m\mid m\in\varphi\}

\displaystyle=\exists m:\varphi.~\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}

Clearly also
\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}\vDash b
and
\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}\vDash\lnot b
. We now complete the derivation:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}\vDash b=\dsser{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}}\odot\dsser{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}\vDash b=\dsser{0}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}\odot\dsser{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}\oplus\mathbf{1}_{\lnot b\textsf{?}m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}_{b\textsf{?}m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~b,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}

Now, suppose
e=u
, so
\varphi\vDash u=u
and
\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~u\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)=m\cdot u
for all
m\in\varphi
and therefore
\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~u,\varphi)=\varphi\odot{u}
. We can complete the proof as follows:

\frac{\varphi\vDash u=u}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~u~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\odot{u}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}

•

C={C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\varphi_{n}(m)
and
\psi_{n}(m)
be defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{post}((\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n},\mathbf{1}_{m})=\mathbf{1}_{\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)}

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime},\varphi_{n}(m))=\mathbf{1}_{\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)}

\displaystyle\psi_{\infty}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle},\mathbf{1}_{m})=\mathbf{1}_{\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)}

Note that by definition,
\varphi_{0}(m)=\mathbf{1}_{m}
,
\varphi=\exists m:\varphi.\varphi_{0}(m)
, and
\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle},\varphi)=\exists m:\varphi.\psi_{\infty}(m)
.

We now show that
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}}
converges (
({\psi}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow{\psi}_{\infty}
). Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for each
n
. That means that
m_{n}=\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)
. Therefore by
Lemma

4.4
we get that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}=\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)
, and therefore
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash\psi_{\infty}(m)
. We now complete the derivation as follows:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Iter}{{Iter}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}

•

C=a
. We assumed that
\Omega
contains all the valid triples pertaining to atomic actions
a\in\mathsf{Act}
, so
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(a,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(a,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

∎

Appendix C
Variables and State

We now give additional definitions and proofs from
Section

5
. First, we give the interpretation of expressions
\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathsf{Val}
where
x\in\mathsf{Var}
,
v\in\mathsf{Val}
, and
b
is a test.

\displaystyle\left\llbracket x\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq s(x)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq v

\displaystyle\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(s)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket E_{1}+E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\llbracket E_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)+\left\llbracket E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket E_{1}-E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\llbracket E_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)-\left\llbracket E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket E_{1}\times E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\llbracket E_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)\cdot\left\llbracket E_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)

Informally, the free variables of an assertion
P
are the variables that are used in
P
. Given that assertions are semantic, we define
\mathsf{free}(P)
to be those variables that
P
constrains in some way. Formally,
x
is free in
P
iff reassigning
x
to some value
v
would not satisfy
P
.

\mathsf{free}(P)\triangleq\{x\in\mathsf{Var}\mid\exists s\in P,v\in\mathsf{Val}.~s[x\mapsto v]\notin P\}

The modified variables of a program
C
are the variables that are assigned to in the program, determined inductively on the structure of the program.

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}(\texttt{skip})

\displaystyle\triangleq\emptyset

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}(C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2})

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{mod}(C_{1})\cup\mathsf{mod}(C_{2})

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}(C_{1}+C_{2})

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{mod}(C_{1})\cup\mathsf{mod}(C_{2})

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}(\texttt{assume}~e)

\displaystyle\triangleq\emptyset

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}\left({C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{mod}(C)

\displaystyle\mathsf{mod}(x\coloneqq E)

\displaystyle\triangleq\{x\}

Now, before the main soundness and completeness result, we prove a lemma stating that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
is valid as long as
P
does not contain information about variables modified by
C
.

Lemma C.1.

If
\mathsf{free}(P)\cap\mathsf{mod}(C)=\emptyset
, then:

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

Proof.

By induction on the program
C
:

•

C=\texttt{skip}
. Clearly the claim holds using
Skip
.

•

C=C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}
. By the induction hypotheses,
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{i}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. We complete the proof using
Seq
.

•

C=C_{1}+C_{2}
. By the induction hypotheses,
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{i}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
. We complete the proof using
Plus
and the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P\Leftrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P
.

•

C=\texttt{assume}~e
. Since
\texttt{assume}~e
can only remove states, it is clear that
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P
must still hold after running the program.

•

C={C}^{\langle e,~e^{\prime}\rangle}
. The argument is similar to that of the soundness of
Invariant
. Let
\varphi_{n}=\psi_{n}=\psi_{\infty}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P
. It is obvious that
({\psi}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow{\psi}_{\infty}
. We also know that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
by the induction hypothesis. The rest is a straightforward application of the
Iter
rule, also using the argument about
\mathsf{assume}
from the previous case.

•

C=x\coloneqq E
. We know that
x\notin\mathsf{free}(P)
, so for all
s\in P
and
v\in\mathsf{Val}
, we know that
s[x\mapsto v]\in P
. We will now show that
P[E/x]=P
. Suppose
s\in P[E/x]
, this means that
s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)]\in P
, which also means that:

(s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)])[x\mapsto s(x)]=s\in P

Now suppose that
s\in P
, then clearly
s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(s)]\in P
, so
s\in P[E/x]
. Since
P[E/x]=P
, then
({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P)[E/x]
=
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P
, so the proof follows from the
Assign
rule.

∎

We now prove the main result. Recall that this result pertains specifically to the OL instance where variable assignment is the only atomic action.
See
5.1

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. By
Theorem

4.7
, we already know that this triple is derivable for all commands other than assignment so it suffices to show the case where
C=x\coloneqq E
.

Now suppose
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq E~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. For any
m\in\varphi
, we know that
(\lambda s.\eta(s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket(s)]))^{\dagger}(m)\in\psi
. By definition, this means that
m\in\psi[E/x]
, so we have shown that
\varphi\Rightarrow\psi[E/x]
. Finally, we complete the derivation as follows:

\frac{\varphi\Rightarrow\psi[E/x]\quad\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi[E/x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq E~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assign}{{Assign}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq E~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

(\Leftarrow)

The proof is by induction on the derivation
\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. All the cases except for the two below follow from
Theorem

4.3
.

–

Assign
.
Suppose that
m\vDash\varphi[E/x]
. By the definition of substitution, we immediately know that

(\lambda s.\eta(s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket(s)]))^{\dagger}(m)\in\varphi

Since
\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket(s)=\eta(s[x\mapsto\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket(s)])
, we are done.

–

Constancy
. Follows immediately from
Lemma

C.1
and the soundness of the
Conj
rule.

∎

Appendix D
Subsumption of Program Logics

In this section, we provide proofs for the theorems in
Section

6.1
. Note that the following two theorems assume a nondeterministic interpretation of Outcome Logic, using the semiring defined in
Example

3.7
.

See
6.1

Proof.

We only prove that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
iff
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
, since
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
iff
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\{}}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\}}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\{}}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\}}}
is a well-known result
(
Pratt 1976
)
.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose
\sigma\in P
, then
\eta(\sigma)\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
we get that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(\eta(\sigma))\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q
, which is equivalent to
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\vDash\exists u:U.\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil^{(u)}
. This means that
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q
, therefore by definition
\sigma\in{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
. Therefore, we have shown that
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
.

(\Leftarrow)

Suppose that
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
and
m\vDash P
, so
|m|=\dsser{1}
and
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{[}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{]}}Q
. This means that
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
, so we also get that:

\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m))=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q

This means that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q
, therefore
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

∎

See
6.2

Proof.

We only prove that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
iff
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
, since
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
iff
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\{}\!|}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}|\!\boldsymbol{\}}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\{}\!|}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}|\!\boldsymbol{\}}}
follows by definition
(
Zilberstein
et al. 2023
;
Möller
et al. 2021
)
.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose
\sigma\in P
, then
\eta(\sigma)\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
we get that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(\eta(\sigma))\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q
, which is equivalent to saying that there exists a
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))
such that
\tau\in Q
, therefore by definition
\sigma\in{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
.
So, we have shown that
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
.

(\Leftarrow)

Suppose that
P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
and
m\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so
|m|=\dsser{1}
and
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}C{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}Q
. This means that
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q\neq\emptyset
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
. In other words, for each
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
, there exists a
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))
such that
\tau\in Q
. Since
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m))=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))
, then there is also a
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m))
such that
\tau\in Q
, so
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q
, therefore
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

∎

Appendix E
Derived Rules

E.1.
Sequencing in Hoare and Lisbon Logic

We first prove the results about sequencing Hoare Logic encodings.

Lemma E.1.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Proof.

We first establish two logical consequences. First, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\mathbf{1}_{m}

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\mathbf{1}_{\eta(\sigma)}

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

And also:

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q

\displaystyle\implies{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q

We now complete the derivation.

\frac{\frac{\forall m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\forall\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m).\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Scale}{{Scale}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P.~\textstyle\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.2.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Proof.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftbox}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}

∎

Now, we turn to Lisbon Logic

Lemma E.3.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Proof.

First, for each
m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P
, let
\sigma_{m}
be an arbitrary state such that
\sigma_{m}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
and
\sigma_{m}\in P
. This state must exists given that
m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P
. We also let
u_{m}=m(\sigma_{m})
and note that
u_{m}\neq\dsser{0}
. We therefore have the following consequences:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~\mathbf{1}_{m}

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~(u_{m}\odot\mathbf{1}_{\eta(\sigma_{m})})\oplus\top

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~(u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q)\oplus\top

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q\oplus\top

\displaystyle\implies\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q

\displaystyle\implies{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q

We now complete the derivation as follows:

\frac{\frac{\forall m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Scale}{{Scale}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P.~(u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.4.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Proof.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftdiamond}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}

∎

E.2.
If Statements and While Loops

Lemma E.5.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{\varphi_{1}\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\varphi_{2}\vDash\lnot b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:If}{\textsc{If}}}

Proof.

First note that
\varphi\vDash b
is syntactic sugar for
\varphi\vDash b=\dsser{1}
, and so from the assumptions that
\varphi_{1}\vDash b
and
\varphi_{2}\vDash\lnot b
, we get
\varphi_{1}\vDash b=\dsser{1}
,
\varphi_{2}\vDash b=\dsser{0}
,
\varphi_{1}\vDash\lnot b=\dsser{0}
, and
\varphi_{2}\vDash\lnot b=\dsser{1}
.
We split the derivation into two parts. Part (1) is shown below:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\varphi_{1}\vDash b=\dsser{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\varphi_{1}}\odot\dsser{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\varphi_{2}\vDash b=\dsser{0}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}\odot\dsser{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}

We omit the proof with part (2), since it is nearly identical.
Now, we combine (1) and (2):

\frac{\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~(\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C_{1})+(\texttt{assume}~\lnot b\fatsemi C_{2})~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Plus}{{Plus}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

∎

Lemma E.6.

The following inference is derivable:

\frac{\varphi\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:If1}{\textsc{If1}}}

Proof.

\frac{\frac{\varphi\vDash b\hskip 8.19447pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.19447pt\dsser{0}\odot\top\vDash\lnot b\hskip 7.97224pt\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 6.80565pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.97224pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Scale}{{Scale}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\oplus(\dsser{0}\odot\top){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\oplus(\dsser{0}\odot\top){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:If}{{If}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.7.

The following inference is derivable:

\frac{\varphi\vDash\lnot b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:If2}{\textsc{If2}}}

Proof.

\frac{\frac{\dsser{0}\odot\top\vDash b\hskip 7.97224pt\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 6.80565pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.97224pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Scale}{{Scale}}}\hskip 7.97224pt\varphi\vDash\lnot b\hskip 7.97224pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\oplus(\dsser{0}\odot\top){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\oplus(\dsser{0}\odot\top){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:If}{{If}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.8 (Hoare Logic If Rule).

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:If (Hoare)}{\textsc{If (Hoare)}}}

Proof.

The derivation is shown below:

\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)\vDash b\hskip-20.48618pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftbox},vdots=3em}\hskip-20.48618pt{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b)\vDash\lnot b\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftbox}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:If}{{If}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.9 (Lisbon Logic If Rule).

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:If (Lisbon)}{\textsc{If (Lisbon)}}}

Proof.

The derivation is shown below:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b\hskip 7.3612pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:If1}{{If1}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftdiamond}}\hskip-49.16684pt\frac{\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash\lnot b\hskip 7.3612pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\lnot b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:If2}{{If2}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftdiamond},vdots=5em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}(P\land b)\vee{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q\vee{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Disj}{{Disj}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.10 (While Rule).

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{({\psi}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow{\psi}_{\infty}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\varphi_{n}\vDash b\quad\psi_{n}\vDash\lnot b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}\oplus\psi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:While}{\textsc{While}}}

Proof.

First, let
\varphi^{\prime}_{n}=\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}
. The derivation has two parts. First, part (1):

\frac{\frac{\frac{\varphi_{n}\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\psi_{n}\vDash\lnot b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\psi_{n}}\odot\dsser{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}

Now part (2):

\frac{\frac{\varphi_{n}\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\varphi_{n}}\odot\dsser{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}\quad\frac{\psi_{n}\vDash\lnot b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Choice}{{Choice}}}

Finally, we complete the derivation as follows:

\frac{\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}\oplus\psi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Iter}{{Iter}}}

∎

E.3.
Loop Invariants

Lemma E.11 (Loop Invariant Rule).

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Invariant}{\textsc{Invariant}}}

Proof.

We will derive this rule using the
While
rule.
For all
n
, let
\varphi_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)
and
\psi_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b)
. We will now show that
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}}
converges. Suppose that
m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b)
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. That means that
\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})\subseteq P\land\lnot b
. We also have that
\mathsf{supp}(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n})=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})
, and since for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})\subseteq P\land\lnot b
, then
\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})\subseteq P\land\lnot b
, and thus
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b)
. We remark that
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)\vDash b
and
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b)\vDash\lnot b
trivially. We complete the derivation as follows:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftbox}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:While}{{While}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Box}(P\land\lnot b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

∎

E.4.
Loop Variants

Lemma E.12.

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{\forall n<N.\quad\varphi_{0}\vDash\lnot b\quad\varphi_{n+1}\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists n:\mathbb{N}.\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Variant}{\textsc{Variant}}}

Proof.

For the purpose of applying the
While
rule, we define the following for all
n
and
N
:

\varphi^{\prime}_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\varphi_{N-n}&\text{if}&n<N\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\text{if}&n\geq N\end{array}\right.\qquad\psi_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\varphi_{0}&\text{if}&n\in\{N,\infty\}\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\lx@intercol\text{otherwise}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}\right.

It is easy to see that
({\psi}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow{\psi}_{\infty}
. Each
\psi_{n}
except for
\psi_{N}
and
\psi_{\infty}
is only satisfied by
\dsser{0}
, so taking
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it must be the case that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}=m_{N}
. By assumption, we know that
m_{N}\vDash\psi_{\infty}
since
\psi_{\infty}=\psi_{N}=\varphi_{0}
.
We also know that
\varphi^{\prime}_{n}\vDash b
and
\psi_{n}\vDash\lnot b
by our assumptions and the fact that
\dsser{0}\odot\top\vDash e=u
for any
e
and
u
.
There are two cases for the premise of the
While
rule (1) where
n<N
(left) and
n\geq N
(right).

\frac{\frac{\forall m<N.\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{m+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\forall n<N.\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N-n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N-(n+1)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{\forall n<N.\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\qquad\qquad\frac{\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Scale}{{Scale}}}}{\forall n\geq N.\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Finally, we complete the derivation.

\frac{\forall N\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:While}{{While}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists n:\mathbb{N}.\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}

∎

Lemma E.13 (Lisbon Logic Loop Variants).

The following inference is derivable.

\frac{\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{0}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash\lnot b\quad\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P_{n+1}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P_{n+1}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists n:\mathbb{N}.\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Lisbon Variant}{\textsc{Lisbon Variant}}}

Proof.

First, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\varphi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}
be defined as follows:

\varphi_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n}&\text{if}&n\leq N\\
\top&\text{if}&n>N\end{array}\right.\qquad\psi_{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}&\text{if}&n\in\{N,\infty\}\\
\top&\lx@intercol\text{otherwise}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}\right.

Now, we prove that
({\psi}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow{\psi}_{\infty}
. Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
m_{N}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}
, then there is some
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{N})
such that
\sigma\in P_{0}
. By definition
(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n})(\sigma)\geq m_{N}(\sigma)>\dsser{0}
, so
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}
as well.
We opt to derive this rule with the
Iter
rule rather than
While
since it is inconvenient to split the assertion into components where
b
is true and false.
We complete the derivation in two parts, and each part is broken into two cases. We start with (1), and the case where
n<N
. In this case, we know that
\varphi_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n}
and
\varphi_{n+1}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n-1}
(even if
n=N-1
, then we get
\varphi_{N}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-(N-1)-1}
).

\hskip-40.00006pt\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{N-n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{N-n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P_{N-n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}\hskip 7.3612pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{N-n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n-1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{N-n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n-1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N-n-1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftdiamond}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Now, we prove (1) where
n\geq N
,
\varphi_{n+1}=\top
.

\frac{\frac{}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

We now move on to (2) below. On the left,
n=N
, and so
\varphi_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}
and
\psi_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}
. On the right,
n\neq N
, so
\psi_{n}=\top
.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{0}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash\lnot b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{0}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P_{0}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assume}{{Assume}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{0}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:liftdiamond}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:True}{{True}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

Finally, we complete the derivation using the
Iter
rule.

\frac{\forall N\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\lnot b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle b,~\lnot b\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Iter}{{Iter}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{N}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists n:\mathbb{N}.\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{n}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\Diamond}P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{{Exists}}}

∎

Appendix F
Hyper Hoare Logic

In this section, we present the omitted proof related the derived rules in Hyper Hoare Logic from
Section

6.2
. First, we give the inductive definition of negation for syntactic assertions below:

\displaystyle\lnot(\varphi\land\psi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\lnot\varphi\lor\lnot\psi

\displaystyle\lnot(\varphi\lor\psi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\lnot\varphi\land\lnot\psi

\displaystyle\lnot(\forall x\colon\mathsf{Val}.\varphi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists x\colon\mathsf{Val}.\lnot\varphi

\displaystyle\lnot(\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall x\colon\mathsf{Val}.\lnot\varphi

\displaystyle\lnot(\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\lnot\varphi

\displaystyle\lnot(\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\lnot\varphi

\displaystyle\lnot(B)

\displaystyle\triangleq\lnot B

F.1.
Variable Assignment

For convenience, we repeat the definition of the syntactic variable assignment transformation.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi\land\psi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi]\land\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\psi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi\lor\psi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi]\lor\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\psi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\forall y:T.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall y:T.\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\exists y:T.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists y:\ T.\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi[E[\sigma]/\sigma(x)]]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi[E[\sigma]/\sigma(x)]]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[B]

\displaystyle\triangleq B

Lemma F.1.

\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi]=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi\}

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\varphi
.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{1}]\land\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{2}]

By the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{1}\}\land\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}\}

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{1}]\lor\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi_{2}]

By the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{1}\}\lor\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}\}

•

\varphi=\forall y\colon T.\ \psi
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\forall y\colon T.\ \psi]

\displaystyle=\forall y\colon T.\ \mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{v\in T}\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\psi[v/y]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\bigcup_{v\in T}\psi[v/y]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\forall y\colon T.\ \psi\}

•

\varphi=\exists y\colon T.\ \psi
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\exists y\colon T.\ \psi]

\displaystyle=\exists y\colon T.\ \mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{v\in T}\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\psi[v/y]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\bigcap_{v\in T}\psi[v/y]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{S})\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\exists y\colon T.\ \psi\}

•

\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi]

\displaystyle=\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\mathcal{A}_{x}^{E}[\varphi[E[\sigma]/\sigma(x)]]

By the induction hypothesis.

\displaystyle=\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\{m\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi[E[\sigma]/\sigma(x)]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\forall t\in\mathsf{supp}(m).~\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\varphi[E[t]/\sigma(x)][t/\sigma]\}

Since
m
and
\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)
differ only in the values of
x
, we can instead quantify over the post-states, which are updated such that
t(x)=E[t]
.

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\{m^{\prime}\mid\forall t\in\mathsf{supp}(m^{\prime}).\ m^{\prime}\in\varphi[t/\sigma]\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\left\llbracket x\coloneqq E\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)\in\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi\}

∎

F.2.
Nondeterministic Assignment

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi\land\psi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi]\land\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\psi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi\lor\psi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi]\lor\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\psi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\forall y\colon T.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall y\colon T.\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\exists y\colon T.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists y\colon T.\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall\langle\sigma\rangle.\ \forall v\colon S.\ \mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi[v/\sigma(x)]]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\varphi]

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists\langle\sigma\rangle.\ \exists v\colon S.\ \mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi[v/\sigma(x)]]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[B]

\displaystyle\triangleq B

Lemma F.2.

If
m\in\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi]
, then
\bigoplus_{v\in S}\mathbf{1}_{\left\llbracket x\coloneqq v\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)}\Rightarrow\varphi

Proof.

Suppose that
m^{\prime}\vDash\bigoplus_{v\in S}\mathbf{1}_{\left\llbracket x\coloneqq v\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!(m)}
. The proof proceeds by induction on the structure of
\varphi
.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
. We know that
m\in\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi_{i}]
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
, so by the induction hypothesis
m^{\prime}\vDash\varphi_{i}
and therefore
m^{\prime}\vDash\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
m\in\mathcal{H}^{S}_{x}[\varphi_{1}]
, so by the induction hypothesis
m^{\prime}\vDash\varphi_{1}
